# Engineering Guide for Claude Code — OpenEden

Oct 25, 2026 · @toddhow

## Purpose and how to use this guide

This guide tells Claude Code, and any human contributor, how to work on OpenEden: what to build first, where code lives, the rules every change follows, and how each change is verified. What to build and why lives in the design docs (OpenEden Extension System, Eden Studio plan, and CreativeMotion's Living Design Doc and Implementation Doc for the animation side); this guide covers how.

- **What OpenEden is:** a full fork of OpenMW, started from the Jolt physics branch (cykoder's `jolt-physics`, the work behind OpenMW merge request !3919). The decision is CreativeMotion's `docs/decisions/0004-total-fork-of-openmw.md` (toddhow, 2026-10-18); the name OpenEden was chosen on 2026-10-20 and OpenEden is the only host for CreativeMotion (2026-10-23).
- **Placing it:** this guide lives at the repository root as `CLAUDE.md` and is committed, so Claude Code reads it at the start of every session. Design handoffs live in `docs/design/` and are committed; exported copies of the living design docs stay local and are never committed (the same rule as CreativeMotion).
- **Authority:** the design docs decide what the system does; this guide decides how work is done. When they disagree, or when a design doc disagrees with CreativeMotion's guide, stop and ask the project owner (toddhow) rather than choosing.
- **Owner:** toddhow is the architect and final reviewer, and is learning C++ and engine development through this project, so explain non-obvious changes in plain language in each pull request.
- **Updating:** when a decision changes, update the relevant design doc and this guide in the same change, and add a changelog row.
- **Words:** in this guide an **extension** is OpenEden's native code system (decision 0006; older documents say "plugin"). A **plugin** is what inherited code means by the word: an ESP or ESM data file, or a Qt or OpenSceneGraph plugin.

## What is inherited and what is new

OpenEden starts as roughly a million lines of someone else's working engine. Most mistakes in a fork come from treating inherited code as if it were new.

- **Inherited:** everything from OpenMW (`apps/`, `components/`, `extern/`, `files/`, `CI/`, `scripts/`, `docs/source/`), plus the Jolt branch's physics port (`apps/openmw/mwphysics/`, `components/nifjolt/`, `components/physicshelpers/`, `components/resource/physicsshape*`, `extern/CMakeLists.txt`). It works, it has tests, and Morrowind plays on it. Change it only for a reason a task spec states.
- **New:** the extension system, the SDK and contracts, the compatibility packs, Eden Studio, CreativeMotion's integration into the engine (decision 0001), and the hosting of the other feature extensions. New code follows this guide in full.
- **Default behaviour is Morrowind's:** with every new feature switched off, OpenEden plays Morrowind exactly as the fork point did. Anything that changes gameplay, balance, animation or physics feel ships behind a setting that is off by default, and "unchanged when off" is verified by comparison, not assumed.

## Scope of the first build

**First build: a fork that builds, tests and plays**

- The Jolt branch finished to the standard of OpenMW's own review (Track J below)
- The fork repository set up, building locally and in CI (M7)
- The extension system's registry and lifecycle, in process (extension phase P1)

**Next:** the move to Vulkan on VulkanSceneGraph (Track V, decision 0002) was meant to come before other work, so new features are built on it once. **Track V was closed by toddhow on 2026-11-10** ("mark it complete, I'm tired of it blocking; if we have issues we will tackle them when they come up"), so other work no longer waits for it; its unrun checks are listed under Track V as follow-ups. Next: CreativeMotion's in-game milestones from M10 inside the engine (decision 0001), the extension system working in the game (Track W, merged), the rest of the extension system with the Morrowind compatibility pack as its first real client, then Eden Studio.

**Not yet**

- Eden Studio beyond Phase 3 of its plan: the shell, viewport, Jolt world, extension host, Editor Module API 1.1 and two core modules are built (see the Eden Studio section below); the next step is task 0091 (API 1.2, draft, awaiting toddhow). The order "after the extension system is done, plan brought up to date first" (toddhow, 2026-10-31) is met: the extension system is done and the plan was refreshed on 2026-11-06
- Compatibility packs for any game other than Morrowind
- Hot reload of extensions
- A stable C ABI for third-party extensions (extension phase P6)
- An OpenGL fallback, a DirectX backend or a renderer abstraction layer (decision 0002: none)
- New renderer features (PBR, ray tracing) beyond what the Vulkan move brings, which were held until Track V was done; Track V is closed (toddhow, 2026-11-10), so the hold is lifted and they proceed through task specs. The proposal is `docs/research/0008-modern-rendering-proposal.md` (nothing decided); toddhow's order is V6, then the Track W merge, then its stages F0 (GPU timing, a heavy benchmark) and F1 (the Slang toolchain, the Vulkan floor) alongside Eden Studio's first phase

| Language | C++20 | `CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 20` in the root `CMakeLists.txt`; matches CreativeMotion |
| Build | CMake (minimum 3.16), Ninja or MSBuild on Windows, Make or Ninja on Linux | Out-of-source builds only; the install prefix must never be inside the build directory (see Pitfalls) |
| Physics | [Jolt Physics](https://github.com/jrouwe/JoltPhysics) v5.5.0, fetched by `extern/CMakeLists.txt`, double precision | Pinned by tag and SHA-512; one Jolt world per session, owned by the core |
| Windows dependencies | vcpkg package set from OpenMW's deps builds (`CI/deps_versions.msvc.sh`, `VCPKG_TAG`), Qt 6.6.3 through aqtinstall | `CI/before_script.msvc.sh` is the reference; local copies go in `deps/` (git-ignored) |
| Linux dependencies | Ubuntu 24.04 packages from `CI/install_debian_deps.sh`, plus a Vulkan loader and driver to run | Uses OpenMW's PPAs; no OpenSceneGraph or COLLADA-DOM packages since task 0014 |
| Rendering | Vulkan on VulkanSceneGraph (VSG) with vsgXchange, and vsgQt in the editor (decision 0002, Track V), always fetched at pinned versions by `extern/CMakeLists.txt` (`docs/dependencies.md`) | No OpenGL fallback, no DirectX, no abstraction layer; OpenSceneGraph is gone from the source and the build (task 0014). OpenMW's prebuilt Windows vcpkg set still contains OSG; nothing uses it |
| Math | VSG math engine-wide (decision 0002, task 0013), with OSG's exact behaviour in `components/misc/vsgmath.hpp` where VSG's differs; CreativeMotion keeps `opas::math` | `CI/check_no_osg.sh` fails on any OSG include or `osg*` namespace in any C or C++ file |
| UI | MyGUI in game, Qt 6 in the launcher, wizard and editor | Inherited |
| Scripting | Lua (LuaJIT) through OpenMW's Lua host | Lua APIs come only from the core; extensions can only publish events the core forwards to Lua (decision 0003) |
| Authored data | JSON with a canonical writer that round-trips byte for byte | The project's rule for new formats (CreativeMotion decision 0002, extension doc 5.2) |
| Tests | GoogleTest (`components-tests`, `openmw-tests`, `eden-studio-tests`), Lua integration tests (`scripts/integration_tests.py`) | Integration tests need OpenMW's example suite at the revision pinned in `.gitlab-ci.yml` |
| Formatting | clang-format 14 (`.clang-format`, `CI/check_clang_format.sh`), clang-tidy (`.clang-tidy`) | CI uses version 14; newer local versions format some untouched code differently, so only format lines you changed |
| Platforms | Windows (MSVC, x64), Linux (GCC and Clang) | macOS is inherited in CI but not a target until toddhow says so |
| CI | Inherited GitLab CI (`.gitlab-ci.yml`) and GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/`) | Confirm which one OpenEden keeps (Open questions) |

Units and conventions follow OpenMW in inherited code (Morrowind units, Z up). New code that talks to CreativeMotion uses its conventions (meters, kilograms, seconds, radians, right-handed, Z up), and converts at one named boundary, never ad hoc.

## Repository layout

```text
CLAUDE.md                 this guide
apps/
  openmw/                 the game; mwphysics/ is the Jolt physics system
  opencs/                 the editor; becomes Eden Studio's shell (Eden Studio plan, phase 1)
  launcher/  wizard/      Qt tools; their strings are translated (files/lang/*.ts)
  navmeshtool/  physicsobjecttool/  bsatool/  esmtool/  niftest/  essimporter/  mwiniimporter/
  components_tests/  openmw_tests/  opencs_tests/  benchmarks/
components/               shared engine libraries (resource, nif, nifjolt, physicshelpers, detournavigator, lua, vfs, ...)
  extension/              the extension loader, registry, lifecycle and core services behind the host API
  creativemotion/         CreativeMotion, compiled into the engine (decision 0001); part of OpenEden, edited here through OpenEden task specs (decision 0010)
  inertialmotion/         inertial locomotion state (docs/design/inertial-locomotion.md)
sdk/                      the SDK: host API headers, contract headers (extension doc 7.1)
extensions/<id>/          first-party extensions, each built against the SDK only (extension doc 5.1, 7.2); compat_morrowind first
game_packs/<id>/          game packs, one game's rules (decision 0013, confirmed 2026-11-06; tasks 0081 to 0086; not created yet)
extern/                   fetched and vendored third-party code (Jolt, recastnavigation, MyGUI, ...)
files/                    data shipped with the engine: shaders, Lua, translations, settings defaults
docs/
  source/                 OpenMW's user and modding documentation (Sphinx)
  design/                 design handoffs (extension system, Eden Studio, inertial locomotion)
  decisions/              decision records, NNNN-title.md
  tasks/                  task specs, NNNN-title.md
  research/               research notes
  credits-vsgopenmw.md    vsgopenmw's authors and licence note (the renderer's origin, decision 0002)
CI/  .gitlab-ci.yml  .github/workflows/   build, test and check scripts
scripts/                  integration tests and developer scripts
```

Each area that gets new OpenEden code gets its own short `CLAUDE.md` describing its rules and pitfalls; create it the first time a task changes that area (for example `apps/openmw/mwphysics/CLAUDE.md`).

## Architecture rules and module boundaries

The extension design doc's dependency rules (section 3.1) are this project's architecture. Until the SDK exists, new code is written so it can move behind it without a rewrite.

```text
hosts (openeden, Eden Studio, test host) ──► core
extensions ──────────────────────────────────► SDK (host API + contract headers) only
compatibility pack ───────────────────────► SDK; provides openeden.game
core ─────────────────────────────────────► no extension, ever
```

- **The core is groundwork:** physics on Jolt, rendering, platform, input, audio, VFS and resources, world and entities, frame loop, Lua host, saves. It names no feature and no game. A feature or a game rule never moves into the core to make an extension's life easier; the host API grows instead.
- **Contracts only:** an extension never includes another extension's headers, links its library or shares globals with it. It asks the registry for a versioned contract.
- **Contracts carry no implementation and no third-party types** (no Jolt, OSG, MyGUI or Qt types in a contract signature).
- **One Jolt world:** the core owns it; extensions create bodies and constraints through the physics service and get IDs back, never pointers. Collision layers are registered by name; the core assigns bits.
- **Game rules live in the compatibility pack:** anything specific to Morrowind (records, races, skeleton names, `.nif`/`.kf` rules) moves out of the core into the Morrowind pack step by step, with Morrowind verified to play the same after each step. **Decided (decision 0013, `docs/design/game-packs.md`, confirmed by toddhow 2026-11-06):** a new tier, the **game pack** (manifest `kind: "game"`), carries a game's rules; the per-game compatibility pack stays an extension and adapts data conventions; a common compatibility pack holds the game-neutral formats (ESM/ESP, archives, NIF of any version); one game pack per session; the second game is Skyrim SE.
- **Fixed, ordered phases:** extensions run in the documented phases (extension doc 4.3), in load order; extensions never read a clock and get `dt` and the step index from the core.
- **Core never renders for an extension:** debug drawing is submitted as data (points, lines, labels) and drawn by the core.
- **Extensions do not affect save data** (decision 0005, toddhow 2026-10-31): they are like script extender plugins; they have no way to write to or read from a saved game. A feature that must live in a save belongs in the core. **Decided (decision 0013, 2026-11-06):** a game pack's state is the game's save data, so game packs (and only they) write save chunks through a handler; decisions 0003 and 0005 stand for extensions.
- **Extensions read physics, never change it, and have no Lua API** (decision 0003, toddhow 2026-10-28): extensions may cast rays and shapes, read body states and receive physics events, and may publish events the core forwards to Lua; they never create, move or step bodies, register collision layers, run in fixed physics steps, or register Lua tables. Any system that must do those things goes into the core.
- **Core feature systems** (decisions 0001 and 0003): CreativeMotion is compiled into the engine; AdvancedBodies and ComplexClothing are planned as core systems too. Reflectron and compatibility packs are extensions. CreativeMotion's own code stays free of engine types; only the engine-side adapters (`apps/openmw/mwrender/creativemotiondriver.*`, `ActorIkController`) touch both. `components/creativemotion/` began as a copy of CreativeMotion's repository and is now part of OpenEden (toddhow, 2026-11-01, decision 0010): its edits and tasks are made in this repository through OpenEden task specs, and the standalone CreativeMotion repository is no longer the source of truth. Its code still stays free of engine types
- **Inherited boundaries stay:** `components/` never includes `apps/`; `apps/openmw/mwbase/` interfaces are how game subsystems reach each other. These are the precedent the registry generalizes (extension doc 3.2).

## Coding standards

Write code a learning C++ developer can follow, and that reads like the code around it.

- **Match the surrounding code:** inherited files keep OpenMW's style (naming with `m` prefixes for members, `camelCase` functions, OpenMW's include grouping); see OpenMW's [Policies and Standards](https://wiki.openmw.org/index.php?title=Policies_and_Standards). New SDK and extension code follows CreativeMotion's standards.
- **No drive-by changes:** do not reformat, rename or "tidy" lines a task does not need to change. Unrelated changes make upstream merges harder and hide the real change in review (OpenMW's `CONTRIBUTING.md` says the same).
- **Ownership:** RAII everywhere; no raw `new` or `delete` in new code; `std::unique_ptr` by default, `std::shared_ptr` or `vsg::ref_ptr` only where the surrounding system already uses it, with a comment saying why. Every new class states who owns it.
- **Engine and physics objects:** store handles or IDs (`JPH::BodyID`, entity IDs), never raw pointers, to anything the engine or Jolt can destroy; look them up when used.
- **Threading:** the physics system runs actor simulation on async worker threads (`apps/openmw/mwphysics/mtphysics.cpp`). Code called from a physics step must not move bodies other threads may be querying, take the main thread's locks, or touch game objects without the existing synchronization. Say in the pull request which thread each new callback runs on.
- **Hot paths:** no heap allocation in per-frame or per-physics-step code; preallocate and reuse buffers.
- **Determinism:** no wall-clock time, no unseeded randomness, no iteration over unordered containers where order affects results, in simulation code and in extension load, phase and event order.
- **Jolt usage:** include `<Jolt/Jolt.h>` before any other Jolt header, in its own include block; use `JPH::RVec3`/`RMat44` for world positions (double precision is on); never construct a Jolt shape whose dimensions can be smaller than its convex radius; prefer `JPH::RayAABox` and similar geometry helpers over building temporary shapes.
- **Errors:** loaders return clear, located errors (file, line, field); no exception crosses the core/extension boundary in either direction (extension doc 6.8).
- **Style:** clang-format 14 and clang-tidy clean on changed lines; builds with warnings as errors on Linux (`-Werror`, as CI's `Ubuntu_GCC` job) and with no new warnings on MSVC (`/W4`); `const` by default.
- **Tuning values:** no magic numbers in new code; defaults live in settings, data files or named constants with a comment on their source. Inherited Morrowind values (GMSTs, vanilla formulas) keep their existing source.
- **User-visible strings:** new game UI strings go through OpenMW's YAML localisation; new Qt strings need the translation files regenerated (`CI/check_qt_translations.sh` fails otherwise).

## Upstream merges and the Jolt branch

Merges are where this codebase has already lost work: the Jolt branch's last two merges of OpenMW master silently reverted at least ten upstream fixes (#8303, #8414, #8206, #8847 and more; repaired in commit 5459a65292). Every merge follows these rules.

- **Merging upstream is optional, never casual:** merge OpenMW master only through a task that names the target commit.
- **Resolve conflicts against upstream's intent:** for each conflicted hunk, read the upstream commit that changed it (`git log -p <base>..<upstream> -- <file>`) and keep its behaviour, re-expressed for Jolt where needed. Never resolve a conflict by taking the fork's old version of a file wholesale.
- **Audit after every merge:** list the lines upstream added since the previous merge base that upstream still has but the fork lacks, per changed file, and account for every one in the pull request (kept, ported to Jolt, or dropped on purpose with a reason). A merge without this audit does not merge.
- **Re-run everything:** unit tests, the Lua integration tests and a short in-game check of the areas the merge touched.
- **Never push to OpenMW:** OpenEden does not open merge requests or push branches to `OpenMW/openmw`. Work that would help upstream is offered by toddhow, not by an agent.
- **Branches (toddhow, 2026-11-06):** `main` is the branch we work on and push to (the GitHub default branch, set in the repository settings by toddhow). `master` is the clean record of OpenMW: it holds only upstream OpenMW commits, nothing of ours is ever committed to it, and it is refreshed only by fetching OpenMW's master and fast-forwarding (never merge, rebase or cherry-pick our work into it; protect it on GitHub against pushes and force pushes). Upstream OpenMW enters `main` only through a task that names the target commit (the merge rules above). `vulkan` is the old name of `main` (same commits), kept until 2026-11-13 and then deleted by toddhow's word; until then each gated push goes to both. Local branch names follow the remote ones.

## Agent workflow and task spec template

Every change follows the same loop, with tests written independently of the implementation and a fresh reviewer before toddhow.

1. **Spec:** write or confirm a task spec in `docs/tasks/` using the template below; it links the design doc section it implements and names the upstream or inherited code it touches by file and function.
2. **Tests first:** a test-writing subagent writes tests from the spec alone, without seeing any implementation.
3. **Implement:** the implementing agent makes the tests pass. It may not edit or delete tests without toddhow's approval; if a test looks wrong, it stops and says why.
4. **Review:** a fresh reviewing subagent that did not write the code checks the diff against the spec, with extra attention to lifetimes, threading, determinism, allocations in hot paths, and behaviour with the feature switched off, and that every new user-visible setting is in the living test profile (`tools/testprofile/profile.cfg` or a variant, with a comment and a README line, or on `exclude.txt` with a reason).
5. **Verify:** all quality gates pass; physics or gameplay changes also get an in-game check with the steps written in the pull request.
6. **Explain and merge:** the pull request explains the change in plain language for toddhow, who approves the merge.

**Full loop required** for every Track V stage (the renderer and the math change; Track V is closed, the rule stands for any follow-up of its open items), anything under `apps/openmw/mwphysics/`, the CreativeMotion adapters and refreshes of `components/creativemotion/`, `components/nifjolt/`, `components/physicshelpers/`, `components/resource/physicsshape*`, the extension loader, registry, SDK and contracts, the Eden archive container reader and writer and the VFS code that reads packs (specs 0050 to 0052; they handle untrusted input), save games, and upstream merges. These carry the determinism, lifetime, threading and compatibility risks, so they never use a lighter loop.

**Lighter loop:** available only for tasks outside those areas (for example `CI/`, `scripts/`, documentation, launcher text) and only when toddhow marks the spec as lighter. No separate test-writing subagent; the implementing agent writes focused tests itself where they are cheap; a fresh reviewing subagent runs once per milestone. toddhow still approves each spec before implementation. A task that touches both areas uses the full loop.

Rules for every session:

- One task per branch; small pull requests.
- Ask when the spec is ambiguous instead of guessing.
- Never invent an API, file format detail or engine fact; cite a file and function at the current commit, a Jolt documentation page, or ask.
- Never commit, push, rebase shared branches or delete branches unless toddhow asks in that session.
- Keep each area's `CLAUDE.md` current with rules and pitfalls learned.
- **Keep the living test profile current:** every task that adds a user-visible setting adds it to `tools/testprofile/profile.cfg` (or a `variants/*.cfg` overlay when it excludes another setting or costs a lot) with a `# task NNNN: what it shows` comment and a feature line in `tools/testprofile/README.md`; debug views, tuning values and bake parameters go on `tools/testprofile/exclude.txt` with a reason instead. `tools/testprofile/coverage.sh` lists every `[Rendering]`, `[WorldLod]`, `[Bodies]`, `[Shadows]` and `[Game]` creativemotion/inertial/sheathing setting that is in neither, and exits non-zero; run it (it takes a fraction of a second) before the pull request. toddhow tests with `tools/testprofile/prepare.sh <config folder>`, which never writes to his real config folder.
- When a change affects CreativeMotion's assumptions (the physics interface, collision layers, frame phases, the event bus), say so in the pull request and update `docs/design/` in the same change.

```markdown
# Task: <short name>
Design doc section: <link>
Goal: <one sentence>
Inherited code touched: <files and functions, at commit <hash>>
Inputs / outputs: <types, files, interfaces touched>
Constraints: <determinism, allocation, threading, off-by-default setting>
Acceptance criteria:
- [ ] <testable statement>
- [ ] With the feature off, <what stays exactly as before and how it is compared>
Out of scope: <what not to change>
```

## Testing and quality gates

A pull request merges only when every gate below passes; gates are added as the code they check comes to exist.

| Gate | What it checks | Runs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Build | Compiles on Windows (MSVC) and Linux (GCC, Clang); `-Werror` on Linux, no new MSVC warnings | Every pull request |
| Format and lint | clang-format 14 clean (`CI/check_clang_format.sh`), clang-tidy clean on changed files | Every pull request |
| Unit tests | `components-tests`, `openmw-tests`, `eden-studio-tests` | Every pull request |
| Integration tests | `scripts/integration_tests.py` against the example suite at the pinned revision, all Lua API tests passing | Every pull request touching `apps/openmw`, `components`, `files` or `scripts` |
| Sanitizers | AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer test runs on Linux (inherited `Ubuntu_GCC_tests_asan`, `_ubsan` jobs) | Every pull request touching C++ |
| Translations | Qt `.ts` files match `lupdate` output (`CI/check_qt_translations.sh`) | Every pull request touching launcher, wizard or editor strings |
| Off means unchanged | With every new setting at its default, the affected behaviour matches the fork point (integration tests, a recorded comparison or a stated in-game check, as the spec says) | Every pull request adding a setting |
| Merge audit | Every upstream line dropped by a merge is accounted for (see Upstream merges) | Every upstream merge |
| Extension boundaries | Extensions include only SDK and contract headers; the core starts with zero extensions | Once the SDK exists |
| Determinism | A scenario run twice gives identical results, also with extensions discovered in a different order | Once the extension host exists |
| Allocation | Phase callbacks, per-step contract calls and event publishing allocate nothing | Once the extension host exists |

Golden data (recordings, expected outputs) changes only in a pull request that says why, reviewed by toddhow.

## Milestones with acceptance criteria

Work them in order unless toddhow says otherwise. Each gets a task spec with its own numbers before work starts.

### Track J: finish the Jolt branch

- [x] MR !3919 review threads resolved: actor shape ratio check, `isAreaOccupiedByOtherActor` radius, broadphase layer names (commit 5459a65292)
- [x] Upstream fixes lost in the branch's merges restored (#8303, #8414, #8206, #8847, scene, cell preloader, projectile manager; 5459a65292)
- [x] Actors spawned inside each other separate (placeatpc), matching Bullet's actor-overlap rules (5459a65292)
- [x] Builds on Windows (MSVC) and Linux (GCC with CI's flags); unit tests and all 32 Lua integration tests pass, including `mwscript magic interactions` (2026-10-18, locally)
- [ ] In-game testing of the MR's tester list: flying over the world, backface-collision mods and moving platforms, a short play-through
- [ ] The actor collider shape drawn by `tcb` checked against the configured `actor collision shape type`
- [ ] The launcher's physics threads tooltip no longer mentions Bullet, with the translation files regenerated
- [ ] The integration tests run in OpenEden's CI

## Definition of done

A task is done only when all of these hold:

- Every acceptance criterion in its spec is met and checked by a test or a written, repeatable in-game check
- All quality gates pass
- With every new setting at its default, Morrowind behaves as before, and the pull request says how that was compared
- A fresh reviewing subagent found no unresolved issues
- The area's `CLAUDE.md` and any affected design doc are updated
- The pull request explains what changed and why in plain language
- toddhow approved the merge

## Guardrails

These rules protect the project legally and keep it honest; breaking one blocks a merge.

- **GPL-3.0-or-later.** OpenEden is a fork of a GPLv3 program; every file stays GPL-compatible, and OpenMW's copyright notices, `AUTHORS.md` entries and licence files are kept. First-party extensions are GPL-3.0-or-later (extension doc 9).
- **Not OpenMW.** OpenEden never presents itself as OpenMW or as endorsed by the OpenMW project, and never pushes to or opens merge requests on OpenMW's repositories.
- **No game assets.** No Bethesda files (`.esm`, `.esp`, `.bsa`, `.nif`, `.kf`, `.hkx`, textures, audio) in the repository; compatibility packs read the user's own installed game data; tests use OpenMW's example suite, generated data or permissively licensed samples with the licence recorded.
- **No proprietary code or SDKs.** Never add the Havok SDK, Creation Kit or game binaries, decompiled game code, or code from mods that are not open-source licensed.
- **Code taken from vsgopenmw keeps its origin.** It is GPL-3.0 like OpenEden; transplanted files keep their copyright notices, the commit message names the vsgopenmw commit they came from, and vsgopenmw's authors are credited (decision 0002).
- **Delirium Physics: ideas only.** The CreativeMotion guardrail applies here too: no code, constants, parameter tables, body layout or data formats from Delirium Physics.
- **Licences checked before adding a dependency.** Record each new dependency's licence and pinned version in `docs/dependencies.md` (create it with the first new dependency); it must be GPL-3.0-or-later compatible.
- **Cite engine facts.** Any statement about OpenMW, Jolt, OpenSceneGraph or Morrowind behaviour cites a file and function at a commit, or a documentation page; invented engine facts are treated as bugs.
- **Original engine behaviour is a feature until proven otherwise.** Follow OpenMW's "original engine fixes" policy in `CONTRIBUTING.md`; a change to vanilla mechanics is a new, off-by-default setting, not a fix.

## Pitfalls already met

Learned while finishing the Jolt branch; each cost real time.

- **A contract in an anonymous namespace.** Each library then has a type of its own. It works with MSVC; GCC sees no implementation in the calling library and calls the pure virtual function directly, which aborts. Contracts go in a named namespace.
- **Windows parallel builds run out of memory.** MSVC with precompiled headers fails with "compiler is out of heap space" at the default parallelism on this machine; build with `-j 8`. In Docker, the same build at `-j $(nproc)` crashed Docker Desktop's engine; use `-j 8` and `--memory 12g`.
- **Never install into the build directory.** OpenEden's Windows install rule copies the build directory's runtime files, so an install prefix inside it copies itself recursively (`install/install/install/...`). Install to a separate folder.
- **Windows runtime files.** A runnable Windows build needs the vcpkg `bin` DLLs, `bin/Release/MyGUIEngine.dll` and Qt deployed with `windeployqt` (the `osgPlugins-3.6.5` folder is no longer needed); without MyGUI the tests exit with `0xC0000135`.
- **Integration tests on Windows (resolved on the Vulkan build).** On the OSG build, vcpkg's OpenSceneGraph crashed in `osg::Image`'s copy constructor while loading the example suite's COLLADA player model. The Vulkan build reads COLLADA with assimp, and all 32 tests pass on Windows (2026-10-29) when the test data's line endings are normalised to LF (see Line endings); there is no Python in the Windows setup, so the runner was a shell script in the session's scratchpad. CI still runs them on Linux.
- **Stale build folders hide missing files.** A build folder keeps files an earlier configuration copied: `lib/material/parallax.glsl` was included by the game's shaders but not installed, and worked only from an OSG-era copy (task 0014, D1). Before calling a dependency or install change done, configure a new build folder.
- **vcpkg's OSG headers are on the include path on Windows.** OpenMW's prebuilt dependency set puts OSG in the same `include` folder as everything else, so an `#include <osg/...>` still compiles there; `CI/check_no_osg.sh` catches it, the Windows build does not.
- **assimp misreads Blender's COLLADA skins.** assimp 6.0.5 does not skip whitespace at the start of `<vcount>`, which shifts every vertex's bone weights by one vertex; models look right in the bind pose and stretch into spikes when animated. `Resource::readAssimpScene` removes that whitespace; read COLLADA only through it.
- **Line endings.** The Windows checkout uses `core.autocrlf=true`, so shell scripts, Python scripts and text test data have CRLF endings there; normalize them before running in Linux, or the `vfs` Lua test fails on `"1\r"`.
- **Build and run deps conflict on Ubuntu.** Installing CI's build groups together with `openmw-integration-tests` fails with held packages; install the build groups and add only xvfb, python3 and git-lfs.
- **Moved checkouts.** CMake caches record absolute source paths; after moving or renaming the checkout, reconfigure (or delete the build directory) before building.
- **Morrowind config import.** A launcher setup that skipped the `Morrowind.ini` import has no `fallback-archive=` lines and shows a magenta menu; `openmw-iniimporter` fixes it but resets the content list, so restore the user's plugins afterwards.
- **vsgopenmw predates VSG 1.1.** Several VSG 1.1 changes fail silently in vsgopenmw code: compile views registered before `Engine::setup` get no transfer task (geometry never uploads), shared reads clone "dynamic" objects (animation roots lose their bones), `vec3 * mat4` divides by the projective term, and `ViewDependentState` gained hooks that must be overridden, and `ArrayState::clone` became `cloneArrayState`, which left intersectors with no vertices. vsgXchange 1.1 also reads TGA, PNG, JPG and BMP as sRGB, which darkened most Morrowind textures until `vsgUtil::removeSrgb` (all image reads go through `vsgUtil::readOptionalImage` or `readImageFromMemory`). Research note 0003 section 5.4 lists each; suspect this first when something renders black or not at all.
- **Debugging the VSG build on Windows.** No Vulkan validation layers are installed. Crashes write `openmw-crash.dmp` to the user data folder; read it with a dbghelp stack walker against the PDBs in `build-jolt`. Capture frames with `--auto-screenshot N --auto-quit M` rather than screen grabs. Interiors are one-sided, so a camera outside the room shows only effects.

- **Slang loads its helper libraries by bare name.** `slang-glslang` (the optimiser) and `slang-glsl-module` are opened later through the process's default DLL search, which does not contain `resources/slang`: without them loaded by full path first, the compile succeeds with different, smaller code, or Slang writes `slang-glsl-module.bin` into the install folder (`DynamicCompiler::load`, `files/shaders/CLAUDE.md`). The loader now refuses to start when either does not load, and the large self-test sees the missing optimiser. A test that needs to show a missing glslang must run in its own process (a load by bare name returns the module the process already has): `SlangIsolated.child_of_the_self_test_without_glslang`. Two builds sharing one shader cache folder delete each other's entries every run: scripts name their own with `OPENEDEN_SHADER_CACHE`.
- **VSG's math reads like OSG's and is not.** `*` between vectors, matrix order, `normalize` of a zero vector, a matrix `==` with a NaN, the sixteen-number constructor's order: use `Misc::VsgMath` wherever OSG's answer matters. `vec * vec` of the same type is deleted so that it cannot compile silently; matrix products are not. The expected values in `apps/components_tests/math` are OSG's recorded results and cannot be regenerated: never edit a `*.golden.inc`.
- **Input arrives while the game loads.** Loading screens pump SDL events and every input handler passes them to Lua, so the Lua manager is created before the input manager (`Engine::prepareEngine`), and `MWBase::Environment` is the Engine's first member so it is destroyed last: `Environment::get()` is null once it is gone, and a movie still playing at exit looks the sound manager up through it. Both crashed the game on toddhow's first launch (`cca74d7fae`). Posting `WM_LBUTTONDOWN` to the game's window during start-up, and a `WM_CLOSE` during the intro movie, reproduce them without a real mouse.
- **The render areas follow the swapchain's extent, not the SDL resize event.** `RenderManager::syncExtents` applies it each frame, because the swapchain is rebuilt only when the next image is acquired and a render area wider than the framebuffer draws nothing (task 0012 stage log, `components/render/CLAUDE.md`). A render graph that has a window or framebuffer keeps VSG's `windowResizeHandler` unless it is set to null, and rescales an area that is already right.
- **Check scripts take Windows paths.** `tools/extension_*_check/check.sh` write their arguments into config files the game reads, so pass `I:/...` or `cygpath -m` for the suite and the work folder; Git Bash paths fail with "the content file does not exist".
- **A crashed game shows a Fatal Error box on the real screen, unless `OPENEDEN_QUIET_WINDOW` is set.** With the variable set (the `tools/` check scripts set it) the crash catcher writes `<app>-crash.dmp` as before, to the log folder (the user profile folder if the crash comes before `Debug::setupLogging`), shows no box and the process exits with code 1; an exception that ends the program shows no box either (`Debug::wrapApplication`), and a run that freezes (the monitor's unchanged test, `IsHungAppWindow`) is answered as the freeze box's Abort button answers it: one log line, `<app>-freeze.dmp`, and the process is ended with exit code 0xDEAD (57005) (`components/crashcatcher/`, 2026-11-04; Linux not checked). Without the variable the box waits in front until someone closes it: a script that starts the game or the editor without it should stop and kill its own run at the first `.dmp`, and only click or resize a window titled for the program, never the dialog that becomes the process's main window after a crash.
- **GCC `-Werror` traps that MSVC never shows.** The Linux gate failed four times in two days on test code that built on Windows: an `if` or `else` whose body is a gtest macro without braces (`-Wdangling-else`: the macro is itself an if/else); a reference bound to a temporary returned by reference (`-Wdangling-reference`); a lambda parameter named `info` inside `INSTANTIATE_TEST_SUITE_P` (`-Wshadow` against gtest's own `info`); `for (const std::string name : vectorOfStrings)` (`-Wrange-loop-construct`: bind by reference); helpers used only under one branch of an `#if` (`-Wunused-function`); `?:` with an enum constant on one side and an integer type on the other (`-Wextra`: for example `VkAccessFlags{ 0 }` against `VK_ACCESS_MEMORY_READ_BIT`, `a9e5cc8879`; give both arms the Flags type). Brace every if/else body, bind loops by reference, and run the Linux gate before pushing.

| 2026-11-10 | **Neutral world model, stage N3a-2 (task 0105; decision 0016; same hand wrote tests and code, no fresh reviewer; Windows only, local commit, not pushed):** the base record and the stub player. `NeutralBase` and `Store<NeutralBase>` (`apps/openmw/mwworld/neutralstore.*`; a `StoreBase`, not a `DynamicStore`, so no loop over the stores, the id index or the save count sees it) are the last element of `ESMStore::StoreTuple`; RefId scheme `<gameId>:b<base>` or `<gameId>:o<placement>`, first description wins, a different later one is a counted conflict, capped (1,048,576). `insertNeutralStubPlayer` (R4, option a: an NPC `Player`, a stub race and class, static records) runs in `World::World` before `ESMStore::setUp()` only with `[Game] neutral world` on and no `Player` in the store; `ESMStore::write` throws while it holds the stub. No `RecNameInts` value, no class, no cell (N3a-3). 31 tests (30 written first: 25 red against stubs, the other 5 test absence; 3 mutations each caught by named tests); `openmw-tests` 1,761 pass. **Morrowind unchanged:** `tools/worldobjects_check` dump (110 lines) and a fixed-time frame SHA-256 identical to the parent executable with the setting off and on, `testesmstorecounts` on the user's Morrowind identical (a first difference was my own test's namespace-scope `RefId`, which interned the spelling `Player` first: no `RefId` at namespace scope in tests). **Real data (aggregates):** the installed Skyrim SE masters, 2,786 cells, 522,288 objects give 14,537 bases, 0 conflicts, 0 refused. | 2026-11-10 | **UI system U1, first slice (task 0104, decision 0015; local commit, not pushed; Windows build only):** RmlUi 6.3 is fetched and pinned (`extern/CMakeLists.txt`, `docs/dependencies.md`: MIT; FreeType is the existing system library, FTL chosen) and built static behind `OPENEDEN_BUILD_RMLUI` (default ON). New `components/ui_rml/` (layer `engine`): a Morrowind bitmap font engine (`.fnt` and `.tex` read through the VFS, linear sampling and nearest at whole scales, every other face to RmlUi's FreeType engine), a VFS file interface and a VSG render interface (scissor and transform, no clip masks, layers or filters). Behind new `[GUI] rmlui` (default false, variant `tools/testprofile/variants/rmlui.cfg`) the engine draws a debug panel (title, live FPS line, list) over the game after MyGUI. **Off is unchanged:** nothing is created; same executable, fixed frame time and seed: 0 of 921,600 pixels differ between two off runs, and the on frame differs only inside the panel rectangle. 72 new `components-tests` pass (bitmap font and geometry red against stubs first; file interface and font engine tests written with the code, one mutation each caught); `openmw-tests` 1,731 pass. **A real MyGUI `ResourceManualFont` fed the way the font loader feeds it fixed two rules:** the first glyph to claim a code point wins and an unmapped code point draws nothing (note 0045's underscore assumption was wrong). Found by running: RmlUi has no default style sheet; vertex buffers must go through `CompileContext::onCompiled`. **Not run:** input, Linux link and run, clang-tidy, a fresh review, GUI scaling, resize with the overlay. The start-up crash seen once with the overlay on was found and fixed: a race already in the engine between `Viewer::compile` (main thread) and a loader thread's compile (access violations in `DescriptorPools::reserve`, also a hang), shown with the overlay OFF plus a 600 ms start-up pause (3 of 30 runs failed); `vsgUtil::viewerCompileMutex()` now keeps them apart (loader compiles shared, `Engine::compile` exclusive; 4 new tests); after it 200 of 200 runs clean (overlay on, off with the pause, on with the pause) against 4 in 13 and 3 in 30 before. GCC 13 `-Werror` syntax check of all new files passes (one dangling-else fixed). Credits for RmlUi and FreeType in `AUTHORS.md`. |
| 2026-11-10 | **Order dependence in `openmw-tests` removed (test fixtures and the test main only; no production code, no golden or recorded expectation changed; Windows only, local commit, not pushed; same hand found the causes and wrote the fixes, no fresh reviewer).** `--gtest_shuffle --gtest_random_seed=77` failed 7 tests (`ItemClassTest.miscellaneous_table`, `JusticeTest.trespassing_in_front_of_an_alarmed_victim_adds_the_trespass_bounty`, `JusticeTest.commit_crime_grid_matches_the_recording`, four `SaveTest.*` in `testdialoguesave.cpp`; no crafting test failed); of 8 other seeds the old binary failed 4 to 18 tests at 6. **Cause 1 (all the recording failures): `ESM::StringRefId` keeps ONE process-wide, case-insensitive string table and the first spelling interned wins (`components/esm/stringrefid.cpp`, `getOrInsertString`); `ContentFileTest` (`mwworld/teststore.cpp`) loads the content files of the machine's own `openmw.cfg`, so on a machine with Morrowind it interns `Gold_025`, `Intruder`, `Hello` and thousands more with Morrowind's capitals, and a recording that prints an id as text (lower case there) fails whenever it runs after that test (running every suite together with each failing test named `ContentFileTest` as the only culprit).** Fix: `apps/openmw_tests/support/refidcasing.hpp` (`TestSupport::primeRefIdCasing`) and one namespace-scope call in each of three test files that interns the recorded spellings before `main()`. **Cause 2 (found with `--gtest_repeat`, about 1 iteration in 10): the flicker test `NpcPartsTest.an_inventory_light_keeps_flickering_through_clear_controllers_and_a_part_update` (> 10 colours in 30 frames) depends on the process-wide `Misc::Rng` and `std::rand` state (`LightController` phase, `Anim::AutoPlay` random groups).** Fix: `apps/openmw_tests/main.cpp` reseeds both at the start of every test (a gtest listener). **Cause 3 (found by seed 1011, and it fails ALONE): `ActorTest.npc_state_save_golden`; `ActorTest::primeSpeedGmsts` runs only for the process's first test and makes objects with that fixture's reference numbers, so the first test's later objects were numbered 1 higher (703) than in any other test (702, the number in `actors_npc_state.golden.txt`).** Fix: `primeSpeedGmsts` puts `mNextIndex` back. **Proof (a private link: the changed files compiled with another build's flags and linked against its objects and libraries, because the disk was full for a full build; that tree is `afbfee8b9f`, main has since gained only `2df05d11d9`, pure maths in `components`):** the plain run, 36 shuffle seeds (including 77), `--gtest_repeat=3` and `--gtest_repeat=40` all pass (1,720 tests; the one skip is `ExtensionHostTest.engineLogLinesReachExtensionSinksAndTheExistingListenerStillWorks`, which skips itself on a second run by design). **Found, NOT fixed (`components-tests`, an `n3a1` binary, 8,000 tests without the Slang, complement and property suites, seeds 1 and 5 only; seeds 2 and 3 did not run because of a harness error):** `TextureCapReadTest` (up to 11 tests), `ArrayReadThreadsTest.the_result_keeps_the_order_of_the_paths` and `TextureMipsReduction.a_swapped_texture_is_handed_out_reduced_to_later_readers` fail in some orders with images of the wrong size (256 wide with 11 mip levels instead of 64 with 7); the suites `TextureMipsSwapper`, `TextureMipsCachedModels` and `TextureMipsArrays` each make `TextureCapReadTest.a_read_without_a_use_is_never_capped` fail, so look at the global state those leave behind (a cache keyed by file name or a raw `Data*` in `components/vsgutil/shareimage.cpp`, `readimage.cpp` or `texturemips.cpp`, an unverified lead); `CompatMorrowindTest.ManifestValidation` fails whenever the working directory is not the build folder (relative path, not an order effect). An earlier run of seed 1 also showed `SettingsValuesTest` failures that did not reproduce (another session's tree was changing). Not run: the Linux gate beyond GCC 13 `-Werror -fsyntax-only` of the changed files, `components-tests` as a whole, clang-tidy, a fresh review, a full build of the fixed sources |
| 2026-11-10 | **CreativeMotion M10, ragdolls in the game, first slice: a dead NPC falls as a ragdoll (task 0106; full loop with the same hand writing tests and code, no fresh reviewer; Windows only, three local commits, not pushed; off by default behind the existing `[Physics] creativemotion ragdolls`).** `RagdollHost` hosts up to 8 limp humanoid ragdolls for dead actors in the game's one Jolt world (`spawnActor`, `readActor`, `removeActor`, generation-checked handles, kill depth, `OPENEDEN_RAGDOLL_TRACE`), `PhysicsSystem::startActorRagdoll` and friends key them by the actor and `PhysicsSystem::remove` ends them, and `MWRender::ActorRagdollDriver` writes 16 mapped bones through `BonePose` each frame after the animation and IK (offsets captured on the spawn frame so frame one is the animation's pose; the pelvis follows the ragdoll's). `CharacterController::updateRagdoll` starts one ragdoll for an NPC that was seen alive and dies (not the player, not creatures, not a corpse loaded dead). **Findings:** the embedded humanoid rig has 16 bodies and no neck (a driver requiring all 17 mapped bones would have been refused on every real death: bones without a body are skipped now); `vsg::dquat` `*` applies the left operand first (the maths goes through `compose`). 46 new `openmw-tests` (written after the code; six single-line mutations each caught by named tests), `openmw-tests` 1,777 pass, GCC 13 `-Werror -fsyntax-only` clean on the changed and new files, layer exceptions 104. In game (`tools/ragdoll_check`, Seyda Neen, fixed frame time, an Imperial Guard killed by script, frames viewed): the guard crumples, falls and lies on the boardwalk, every body asleep after 676 steps (11.3 s), **two runs give identical traces**; frames are not byte-identical in any pair, the parent executable against itself included (6k to 15k of 480k pixels), so "off is unchanged" is claimed for the code path, not for frames. **Limits:** the ragdoll starts in the rig's standing rest pose, not the actor's pose at death; only the pelvis translates; the capsule, pick and loot box stay where the actor died; no pose in saves (a loaded corpse lies in the death clip's last pose); a bone rebuild ends the ragdoll. **Not run:** the Linux gate, Lua tests, `components-tests`, clang-tidy, sanitizers, a fresh review, creatures, interiors, quickload or cell unload with a live ragdoll, eight at once in game, frame cost. |
| 2026-11-10 | **Inertial locomotion: why feet slide and why the authored clips never play (task 0108, research note 0052; Windows only, local commits, not pushed; full loop in form only: the same hand wrote tests and code, no fresh reviewer):** (1) The clips `walkstart`, `runstart`, `walkstop`, `runstop`, `runskid`, `turnleft90`, `turnright90` exist in no animation source: none in the three Morrowind BSAs or the 163 loose `.kf`, none in the repository (`components/creativemotion/data/clips/humanoid-locomotion.glb` is CreativeMotion's 20-bone sample rig and nothing loads it), so `MWAnim::Play::hasAnimation` is false and the procedural fallback always ran, silently; and the start clip could not be chosen even with data (`CharacterController::preAnimation` required `LocomotionPhase::Starting`, which exists only from the frame after the decision; fixed as `InertialMotion::shouldPlayAuthoredStart`, 4 tests); one Info line per session now lists which of the seven exist. Authoring the clips (toddhow, 2026-11-03: our own) is not done. (2) Two causes of foot sliding, measured with a scripted scenario in ToddTest (`tools/footslip_check`, ankles traced in world space). **Walking:** inertial locomotion moves the body at a constant speed while the clip's root speed varies by 10 to 20 percent over a step; the median of |body speed - root-motion speed| is 11.8 units per second in a walk against 1.9 in vanilla (strafe 18.2 against 4.3). New `[Game] inertial stride sync` (off by default, on in the test profile; `InertialMotion::strideShape`, `Actor::setStrideShape`, `MovementSolver::move`) multiplies the smoothed ground speed by the frame's root-speed ratio: median 3.5 (strafe 7.9), mean ground speed unchanged within 2 percent; not judged by eye. **Stopping:** with `creativemotion preset = natural` (not inertial locomotion alone, which pops instead) the stance foot glides back to idle for about 0.4 s at 75 to 107 units per second, because the pose is blended from the frozen mid-stride walk to idle (0.35 s x 1.5) after the body has stopped; the glide scales with that blend. New `[Game] foot lock` (off by default, needs `foot planting`, on in the test profile; `InertialMotion::FootLock` in `ActorIkController`) holds a planted foot while the body is still and lets the hold drift at 8 units per second: mean stop peak over eight stops 60.8 to 23.6 units per second, ground covered 16.5 to 7.8 units (second run 45.9 to 26.1, 14.0 to 7.7); the stop after a running arc stays at a peak of 94. Frames viewed (`inertia\run10\before_stop_wr.png`, `after_stop_wr.png`): the glide is gone and the character stands in a wide stance for 1 to 2 s, a trade toddhow must judge. Off means unchanged: the player's `CMTRACE` of the vanilla and inertial-only variants over the scenario differs only in the physics position of 1 to 6 of 1893 frames (one physics step, the noise of two runs of one build); with foot planting on and both new settings off, leg frames of the old and new executables differ within the spread of two runs of the new one; the IK path was otherwise checked by reading only. 51 inertial motion tests pass in a private MSVC link (13 of the first 24 new tests red against stubs; two foot lock tests corrected after they failed, one added after a game run showed a stale-position fault). **Not run:** the Linux gate, clang-tidy, the Lua tests, the whole `components-tests` and `openmw-tests`, the authored start clip in a game (no clip exists), NPCs, slopes, first person, a fresh review. Decisions for toddhow in the task |

| 2026-11-10 | **Skyrim SE pack: owner, lock and teleport decoded and filled into the neutral model (task 0099 stage N4, record-decoding half; same hand wrote tests and code, no fresh reviewer; Windows only, local commit, not pushed):** `game_skyrimse` reads XOWN (+XRNK), XLOC and XTEL on REFR and ACHR (`PlacedRef::mOwner/mLock/mTeleport`) and the adapter fills `OwnerComponent`, `LockComponent` and `TeleportComponent`. **Layouts verified on the installed data** (five masters plus the 535 plugins of the MO2 profile Default, aggregates only): XOWN 4 bytes, XRNK 4, XLOC 20 (level @0, key @4, flags @8; the padding bytes 1 to 3 and 9 to 11 hold garbage and are not read; my first survey looked at offset 16 by mistake and a unit test caught it), XTEL 32 (door, position, rotation, flags); no other size, no repeat, XGLB does not occur. Plumbing: `world_view.hpp` stays 1.0; components travel through a pack-internal `ComponentSource` that `SkyrimWorldView` implements (found by `dynamic_cast` in `loadNeutralCell`); the index pass now records each teleport door's cell (also for compressed records) so `destinationCell` is filled; `world_model.hpp` DRAFT 0.2 (`TeleportComponent::flags`). Hostile input: other sizes counted and decode to nothing, repeats keep the first, unresolved ids dropped and counted, hostile XTEL numbers drop the teleport. | 2026-11-10 | **Lua UI compatibility layer surveyed (research note 0045 section 16; docs only, read-only, nothing built or run; every code claim cites file and function, MyGUI/RmlUi memory marked RECALLED-VERIFY; estimates are mine, plus or minus 50 percent):** the hypothesis that MyGUI is only the leaf layer of `components/lua_ui` is **refuted in its strong form**: the layout arithmetic (Flex, Container, relative/anchor placement, Window drag, layout-table diffing, templates, event bubbling) is OpenEden's own integer C++, but every Lua widget class inherits a MyGUI class (`LuaText : MyGUI::EditBox`, `LuaImage : MyGUI::ImageBox`, the rest `MyGUI::Widget`), MyGUI owns the objects and decides type identity, and supplies text extents and wrapping, hit testing, focus, capture, clipping, alpha inheritance, layers, pointer, texture sizes and fonts (235 MyGUI lines in 22 of 31 files). There are no tooltips in the Lua UI. Options: A keep the layout and replace the MyGUI inheritance by a leaf interface on the element protocol, with RmlUi as renderer and hit-tester only (recommended, about 40 to 65 working days); B reimplement the layout in the new library (12 to 20 weeks, not recommended); C translate tables to RmlUi flex CSS (cannot match: `LuaFlex` truncates `grow` per child, floors `stretch`, zeroes the primary axis for `relativeSize` in an auto-sized Flex, and so on; use it for new UI only). Fix: with `taa` on, the default shader's blended, depth-tested pipelines get a slope-scaled depth bias of 1.25 x `taa jitter scale` (`Pipeline::Graphics::overlayDepthBiasSlope`, `usesOverlayDepthBias`, `Render::Taa::overlayDepthBiasSlope`); 9 new tests (3 red first, one added after a first narrower predicate failed in the game); no new setting, `taa = false` has no pipeline change. The earlier row's "needs `ao temporal`" was a confound. Not explained: with the fragment AO pass (`ao` on, `ao temporal` off) the lava is smooth and stable with or without the fix. Not run: the Linux gate, Lua tests, `openmw-tests`, clang-tidy, rain, snow, smoke and water-edge scenes with the bias, a second GPU. |

| 2026-11-10 | **Terrain height ranges for coarse tiles (task 0088 follow-up; Windows only, local commit, not pushed).** Checked whether `Terrain::Builder::getBatchData`'s defect (for a tile of level 3 and up, `numVerts(0.125, lod)` truncates to 1, so every grid cell read only the south-west vertex and the cull box was flat at one corner height) explains the far mountain hole: **it does not.** The cull node is the box's bounding sphere, whose radius is the horizontal half diagonal (46 km for a level 3 tile) and barely depends on the height range, so a flat box moves the sphere centre by half the mountain's height and leaves out a vertex only at a tile corner, by about height squared over twice the radius (315 units for a 5,400 high peak at a corner: about 2.5 pixels at 150 km, where level 3 tiles are chosen at all). It is a real bug and is fixed: new `components/terrain/heightranges.hpp` (`Terrain::computeHeightRanges`: each grid cell takes the vertices from floor to ceil of its span, equal to the old code where the grid divides the vertices, levels 1 and 2 and the live terrain) used by the builder; 5 tests in `testheightranges.cpp` (the old behaviour shown: a 5,000 high mountain gives max 0 with the old loop; the new ranges contain every vertex; levels 1 and 2 unchanged). The game builds and runs the flight scenario with it. Not run: the Linux gate, the Lua tests, the whole `components-tests`, a frame that shows the old box culling anything (none expected). The persistent hole therefore stays attributed to the off-view tile gap of the row below, which toddhow has not yet confirmed. |
| 2026-11-10 | **Far terrain tiles appearing from nothing when the camera turns (task 0088 follow-up; `[WorldLod] map view` only, Windows only, local commit, not pushed; full loop with the same hand writing tests and code, no fresh reviewer).** Cause, found by experiment (an audit of every leaf tile square against what was drawn, and a log of every tile build with whether anything already drew its square): steady scenes are fully covered (0 uncovered leaves at 6 poses and 2 flights, so the haze, the bake, the digest, the residency budget and the hand-over were not the cause), but a tile was chosen only when its sphere was in the view cone (`Lod::evaluateTile` skipped the rest and `mWanted = chosen && (held || visible)`), so after a turn every tile of the newly seen far ground, mountains included, was built from nothing (4 builds a frame, nearest first, each dithering in over the sky) while the object clusters, which have other rules, were already drawn: a 360 degree yaw over 8 s built 45 tiles with nothing beneath against 4 covered. Fix: `SelectionCamera::mOffViewScale` (new, 0 = off, as before): with map view `LodTerrain` sets it to `Lod::offViewThresholdScale` (8), and a tile in the view distance but out of the cone that has no choice yet is chosen with the threshold times 8 (a coarse tile) and stays not visible; a choice made in view stays when the camera looks away (no coarse over fine churn); a chosen tile past the live range is wanted by the planner seen or not, and a not-yet-built finer child past the live range counts as coming for `refinedTileMayGo`. Same 360 degree sweep: 3 empty builds against 45, 37 new tiles against 49 (no churn), flight 0 empty; drawn tiles 8 to 12 MiB, unchanged. 5 new tests in `testtileoffview.cpp` (4 red first against the old selection, the property test shown red too), `Lod*` tile and residency tests 172 pass in a private link. **Not run:** the Linux gate, the Lua tests, the whole `components-tests` and `openmw-tests`, clang-tidy, a fresh review, toddhow's own play (the transient was seen in logs and counts; the frames of a 1 Hz screenshot cannot show it). Found, not changed: `Builder::getBatchData`'s height ranges read only the south-west corner for tiles of level 3 and up (`numVerts(0.125, lod)` truncates to 1), a wrong cull box that no frame showed culling; the earlier far-terrain gap fix (`mLiveRange`, a rough tile Live out to several times the live range) is in his 2026-11-09 build. |

F4: `catchmentRatio` has an overload that divides by the pond footprint of a floor-capped pit (3 to 4 times the whole-pit ratio on real land; the largest pond ratios exceed 2^17 and clip); the flat-tank level formula is unchanged and its under-prediction for bowls is documented (4.9 against 1.0 for a paraboloid, Cmax 12). F5: `FillResult` stores its cap and `Basins` a digest of its fill; `capacityFromFloor` and `catchmentRatio` return structs with an error and refuse finite-cap fills, basins of another fill and wrong sizes. F6: heights beyond +-1e7 are refused, non-finite arguments never mean wet in `coverage`, `damp`, `depthAt`, `levelOf`; `flowDirection` is bounds-checked. 57 tests (14 new), 8 red against the old code, 12 of 12 mutations caught (one only by a compile error), MSVC `/W4 /WX` and GCC 13 `-Werror` pass. Not run: the full Linux gate, clang-tidy, `components-tests` as a whole, a re-review. Details in the stage log of `docs/tasks/0095-puddles-and-wetness-v2.md` |

Facts found: the game enables only four device features (`vsgengine.cpp`, `Engine::createRenderEngine`) while the RTX 4070 report has `multiDrawIndirect`, `drawIndirectFirstInstance`, `drawIndirectCount`, descriptor indexing and mesh shaders; VSG 1.1.16 has `DrawIndexedIndirect` (fixed host-side count, no `...Count` variant); the object shader reads a 128-byte push-constant modelview that VSG forms in double precision, so an indirect path needs instance transforms relative to a chunk origin; there is no bindless (18 bindings per model part), so batches are per model part; actors, controllers, blended and activatable objects (picking uses the render scene graph) stay on the CPU path. | 2026-11-02 | The fast-flight "hang" of the LOD baseline was a crash with the Fatal Error box left on screen (`bf9a21e6a9`): `vsgUtil::DeletionQueue` had no lock, the terrain preload thread (`Preload::setPositions` -> `Paging::updateView` -> `detach`) added to it while the main thread advanced it in `Render::Engine::loop`, corrupting the vector. It now has a mutex and releases objects after the lock is dropped; test `DeletionQueueThreads` crashed every time before. Before the fix the flight crashed at about 8,100 frames; after it 3 of 3 runs finished 22,000+ frames. Still open: `Paging::updateView` edits `rootNode->children` on the worker thread (safe only while that view is not in the scene) and the 25 to 110 ms cell-load hitches. This may also have been behind some of toddhow's earlier crashes |
